/// C `Strat_Perc93`: val * 15/16.
pub fn strat_perc93(val: i16) -> i16 {
    val.wrapping_sub(val >> 4)
}

// ============================================================
// Velocity vector generation (C strat_common.c:111-161)
// ============================================================

/// C `Strat_GenVecs2D` (alvelvecs_l, STRATROU.ASM:100-148): XZ velocity
/// from Y rotation + speed; vy = 0.
pub fn strat_gen_vecs_2d(al: &mut Alien) {
    let angle = al.roty;
    let speed = al.vel as i16 as f32;
    al.vx = (speed * snes_sin(angle)) as i16;
    al.vy = 0;
    al.vz = (speed * snes_cos(angle)) as i16;
}

/// C `Strat_GenVecs3D` (alvel3vecs_l, STRATROU.ASM:221-283): 3D velocity
/// from pitch (negated rotx, ASM convention) and yaw.
pub fn strat_gen_vecs_3d(al: &mut Alien) {
    // ROM `n3dvecs_l` (STRATROU.ASM:316-317) negates YAW (not pitch): vx =
    // -vel*sin(roty)*cos(rotx). The port negated pitch and left yaw, flipping
    // vx's sign -> LEFT/RIGHT steering was inverted (vy stayed correct via the
    // renderer's Y-flip, which is why only X looked wrong). snes_cos is even so
    // negating yaw flips ONLY vx; vy/vz are unchanged. Universal fix (the ROM
    // negates yaw for every object).
    // Bit-exact port of the ROM's fixed-point math (sin/cos tables + mulslog),
    // verified against the real ROM by sf-oracle (tests/gen_3dvecs.rs). The
    // previous float `sin/cos`*multiply drifted ~2% per op and accumulated into
    // visible wobble. Pitch stays negated for the renderer Y convention; cos is
    // even so vx/vz are unaffected, only vy's sign follows the convention.
    use crate::snes_trig::{mulslog, COSTAB, SINTAB};
    let yaw = (al.roty as i8).wrapping_neg() as u8 as usize;
    let pitch = (al.rotx as i8).wrapping_neg() as u8 as usize;
    let vel = al.vel as i32;
    let cosx = COSTAB[pitch] as i32;

    al.vx = mulslog(mulslog(vel, SINTAB[yaw] as i32), cosx) as i16;
    al.vy = mulslog(vel, SINTAB[pitch] as

ASSISTANT (final):

## This round

Migrated every remaining `snes_sin/cos × float-multiply` site to the ROM-exact `snes_trig` (SINTAB/COSTAB + `mulslog`) that the oracle proved bit-exact:

- **`common.rs`** — `gen_vecs_2d`, `gen_side_vecs`, `gen_front_vecs`
- **`path_adapter.rs`** — `genvecs_2d` + `genvecs_3d`
- **`bosses.rs`** — surface-swim wiggle

And the sweep surfaced a **real latent bug**: `path_adapter::genvecs_3d` (the space-level lane) was **missing the yaw negation** — the exact same left/right inversion the oracle caught for the player, still lurking in space levels. Fixed to match the ROM-validated `common.rs`.

Net effect: the ~2% per-op float drift is now gone across **all** object/enemy/boss/path motion — the accumulating wobble should be visibly reduced, and space-level object steering is no longer inverted. Build clean, oracle + steering tests green.

## Where the methodology stands

You now have a self-reinforcing loop, and it's already paid for itself twice (steering-sign confirmation + the wobble root cause + a latent path-lane inversion):
- **Oracle** runs real ROM code as ground truth
- **Differential tests** find discrepancies
- **Fixes port exact ROM math**, proven bit-exact

**Natural next steps** (any of these):
1. Add oracle differential tests for `sidevecs_l`/`frontvecs_l`/`alvelvecs_l` to validate their yaw conventions the same way (I flagged that some ROM variants may negate yaw where the Rust doesn't — worth confirming, not guessing).
2. Extend the oracle to `GameVars_Init` (your original "test the init routine" ask) and `playermove_srou`.
3. **Launch the game** to feel whether motion is now noticeably smoother.

Want me to keep going down the oracle path (1 or 2), or launch it so you can feel the difference?

=== Rust angle function ===
rust/sf-strat/src/common.rs:225:pub fn snes_sin(angle: u8) -> f32 {
rust/sf-strat/src/common.rs:231:pub fn snes_cos(angle: u8) -> f32 {
rust/sf-strat/src/common.rs:445:pub fn strat_angle_xz(src: &Alien, dst: &Alien) -> u8 {
pub fn strat_angle_xz(src: &Alien, dst: &Alien) -> u8 {
    let dx = dst.worldx.wrapping_sub(src.worldx) as f32;
    let dz = dst.worldz.wrapping_sub(src.worldz) as f32;
    let mut angle = dx.atan2(dz);
    if angle < 0.0 {
        angle += 2.0f32 * 3.141_592_65_f32;
    }
    ((angle * (256.0f32 / (2.0f32 * 3.141_592_65_f32))) as i32) as u8
}

### Turn 598 @ 2026-07-04T00:51:51.189Z
USER:
=== Rust strat_chase_proportional ===
pub fn strat_chase_proportional(current: i16, target: i16, shift: u32) -> i16 {
    if current == target {
        return current;
    }
    let diff = current.wrapping_sub(target);
    // adiv2^shift: arithmetic shift toward zero (not toward -inf).
    let mut step = if diff >= 0 {
        diff >> shift
    } else {
        -((-diff) >> shift)
    };
    if step == 0 {
        step = if diff > 0 { 1 } else { -1 };
    }
    current.wrapping_sub(step)
=== ROM s_Achase_var — GSU or 65816? ===

### Turn 602 @ 2026-07-04T00:57:53.763Z
USER:
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""GSU / Super FX (GSU-2) disassembler for the SF2 3D/shape banks.
3	
4	The Super FX instruction set is prefix-modal: opcodes $3D/$3E/$3F (ALT1/ALT2/
5	ALT3) change the meaning of the *following* opcode, and $10-$1F (TO), $20-$2F
6	(WITH), $B0-$BF (FROM) are register prefixes. This decoder tracks the ALT state
7	across a linear stream and selects the correct mnemonic per (opcode, alt).
8	
9	Instruction lengths: most ops are 1 byte; branches ($05-$0F) take a 1-byte
10	signed offset; IBT/LMS/SMS ($A0-$AF) take 1 immediate byte; IWT/LM/SM
11	($F0-$FF) take a 2-byte immediate word. Verified against SF1 GSU source in
12	reference/ultrastarfox/SF/MARIO/*.MC.
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	from dataclasses import dataclass
16	
17	R = lambda n: f"r{n}"
18	
19	# Per-opcode decode. Value can be:
20	#   ("MNEM", extra_bytes)                      -- alt-independent
21	#   {0:(...),1:(...),2:(...),3:(...)}          -- alt-dependent variants
22	# extra_bytes: 0,1,2 ; special 'branch' means 1 signed byte offset.
23	
24	def _reg_ops():
25	    t = {}
26	    # 0x00-0x0F control / branches
27	    t[0x00]=("STOP",0); t[0x01]=("NOP",0); t[0x02]=("CACHE",0)
28	    t[0x03]=("LSR",0); t[0x04]=("ROL",0)
29	    for op,mn in [(0x05,"BRA"),(0x06,"BLT"),(0x07,"BGE"),(0x08,"BNE"),(0x09,"BEQ"),
30	                  (0x0A,"BPL"),(0x0B,"BMI"),(0x0C,"BCC"),(0x0D,"BCS"),(0x0E,"BVC"),(0x0F,"BVS")]:
31	        t[op]=(mn,"branch")
32	    # 0x10-0x1F TO / MOVE(with B)   -- render as TO rN
33	    for n in range(16): t[0x10+n]=(f"TO {R(n)}",0)
34	    # 0x20-0x2F WITH rN
35	    for n in range(16): t[0x20+n]=(f"WITH {R(n)}",0)
36	    # 0x30-0x3B STORE (rN)  ; 0x3C LOOP ; 0x3D/E/F ALT prefixes
37	    for n in range(12): t[0x30+n]={0:(f"STW ({R(n)})",0),2:(f"STB ({R(n)})",0)}
38	    t[0x3C]=("LOOP",0); t[0x3D]=("ALT1",0); t[0x3E]=("ALT2",0); t[0x3F]=("ALT3",0)
39	    # 0x40-0x4B LOAD (rN) ; 0x4C PLOT/RPIX ; 0x4D SWAP ; 0x4E COLOR/CMODE ; 0x4F NOT
40	    for n in rang

ASSISTANT (final):
Big milestone reached. Here's where the GSU emulator stands:

## The GSU emulator runs real ROM code

`rust/sf-oracle/src/gsu.rs` now executes the **actual `mcallarctan16` GSU program from the ROM** end-to-end — reading inputs and writing the result through the real GSU RAM ABI (`m_x1`/`m_y1`/`m_cnt`). And it matches `atan2` **exactly** for every cardinal and diagonal angle:

```
x=100 y=  0: GSU=90°  atan2=90°   dist=0  ✓
x=100 y=100: GSU=45°  atan2=45°   dist=0  ✓
x=-100 y=100: GSU=315° atan2=315° dist=0  ✓
x=100 y=-100: GSU=135° atan2=135° dist=0  ✓
x=-100 y=-100: GSU=225° atan2=225° dist=0  ✓
```

That validates the **entire core** — the prefix-modal engine, ~50 opcodes, memory ops, `FMULT`, `MOVE`/`MOVES`, `LINK`, `LOOP`, branches, `GETB`/`ROMB`, and the RAM/ROM bus. Building toward this validation *itself* found two emulator bugs (`MOVES` direction was backwards; `ROMB` is ALT3 not ALT2) — proving the value of validating against real ROM code.

## The one remaining bug (well-scoped)

Shallow/off-axis angles (e.g. `x=50,y=87` → gives 90° instead of 29°) fail because they use the **shift-subtract divide subroutine at GSU `$8192`** for the fine angle — the cardinal/diagonal cases take a shortcut that skips it. So there's one subtle carry/flag bug in the divide loop (`ROL`/`SUB` carry or `LOOP`/`r13`). That's the single thing between here and a fully-trusted GSU oracle, and I've traced it down to that exact loop in memory.

## Net position

You now have a **complete two-processor ROM oracle**: the 65816 harness (already fixed steering precision/wobble, path-lane inversion, front/side off-by-one) plus a GSU emulator that runs real Super-FX code and is one bug away from validating the angle/3D math too. Everything committed on `wgpu-backend`.

Want me to keep going and crack the `$8192` divide bug (then validate `strat_angle_xz`), or pause here?

### Turn 640 @ 2026-07-04T02:16:15.869Z

The file /home/ben/.claude/projects/-home-ben-src-starfox-hd/memory/rom-oracle-plan.md has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)

ASSISTANT (final):
Major GSU correctness fix landed. Here's the status:

## The delay-slot fix (fundamental)

Digging into the MARIO source (`MMATHS.MC`'s `mdivu3115` divide) revealed the root cause: **the GSU has branch delay slots** — the instruction after a taken `loop`/`jmp` executes *before* the jump. The source's `loop` / `jmp` are each followed by a `rol`/`lsr` whose cycles the comments explicitly count. That trailing `rol` is the per-iteration `r0` shift in the divide loop.

Without delay slots, my emulator skipped it, so the divide's quotient stayed 0 and every off-axis arctan collapsed to 90°. **Implemented delay slots for all branches** — now the divide runs all 16 iterations and produces a real quotient. This is a big accuracy improvement for *any* GSU routine with loops (which is most of them).

Cardinal/diagonal angles are still bit-exact against `atan2`.

## The one remaining arctan bug (well-scoped)

The divide now produces `87/50 ≈ 1.74` for `x=50,y=87` — but that's `>1`, so the arctan table index overflows `ARCTANTAB` and the fine angle comes out wrong (88° vs 29°). It's an octant/scaling detail in the `$8204+` finalize: for `|y|>|x|` the ratio should be `<1` (smaller/larger), but the divide is getting larger/smaller. I've traced it to the exact code range and noted it in memory.

## Where this stands

This is a natural, strong consolidation point after a very long, very productive run. The two-processor ROM oracle is real and has delivered:
- **65816 side**: fixed steering precision/wobble (bit-exact `mulslog`), path-lane inversion, front/side off-by-one — all proven against the ROM
- **GSU side**: a working Super-FX emulator that runs real ROM code, validated exact for cardinal/diagonal arctan, one scoped bug from full angle validation

Everything's committed on `wgpu-backend`. The next session can crack the final arctan octant bug (clean continuation point in memory), then use the GSU oracle to validate `strat_angle_xz` and the 3D transforms — or you could launch the game now to feel the cumulative effect of all the 65816-side fixes. Your call on which direction next.

### Turn 667 @ 2026-07-04T02:44:00.895Z
USER:
=== Rust strat_speed_to ===
pub fn strat_speed_to(al: &mut Alien, target_speed: u8, rate: u8) -> bool {
    if al.vel == target_speed {
        return true;
    }
    if al.vel < target_speed {
        al.vel = al.vel.wrapping_add(rate);
        if al.vel > target_speed {
            al.vel = target_speed;
        }
    } else if al.vel - target_speed < rate {
        al.vel = target_speed;
    } else {
        al.vel -= rate;
    }
    al.vel == target_speed
}
// ============================================================
// Percentage scaling (C strat_common.c:84-107, STRATROU.ASM perc*)
// ============================================================
/// C `Strat_Perc75`: val * 3/4 = val/2 + val/4 (arithmetic shifts).

pub fn strat_apply_velocity(al: &mut Alien) {
    al.worldx = al.worldx.wrapping_add(al.vx);

=== fp16_to_float scale + lerp_cam_angle_f big_jump threshold ===
fn fp16_to_float(val: i32) -> f32 {
    val as f32 / 65536.0
}

// Shortest-path interpolation in the SNES 0-255 angle system, at
fn lerp_cam_angle_f(from: i16, to: i16, t: f32, big_jump: &mut bool) -> f32 {
    let a8 = (from & 0xFF) as i32;
    let b8 = (to & 0xFF) as i32;
    let mut diff = b8 - a8;
    if diff > 127 {
        diff -= 256;
    }
    if diff < -128 {
        diff += 256;
    }
    if diff > 48 || diff < -48 {
        *big_jump = true; // > ~67 deg in one tick: cut
    }
    (a8 as f32 + diff as f32 * t).rem_euclid(256.0)
}

=== find do_coll_l source in ASM ===
reference/ultrastarfox/SF/STRAT/STRATROU.ASM
=== Rust do_coll (coldet.rs 113+) ===
    fn do_coll(&mut self, victim: u16, attacker_ap: u8) {
        let in_tunnel = self.vars.pshipflags3 & PSF3_INTUNNEL != 0;
        let al = &mut self.objs.aliens[victim as usize];
        // Cooldown timer: only apply damage every framesperAP frames.
        if al.collcount > 0 {
            al.collcount -= 1;
            return;
        }
        // Indestructible objects (hardHP) don't take damage.
        if al.hp == 0xFF {
            al.collcount = FRAMESPERAP;
            return;
        }
        let mut damage = attacker_ap;
        // In tunnel mode, halve hardAP damage.
        if in_tunnel && damage == HARD_AP {
            damage >>= 1;
        }
        if al.hp <= damage {
            al.hp = 0;
        } else {
            al.hp -= damage;
        }
        al.collcount = FRAMESPERAP;
    }

ASSISTANT (final):
The disassembly reveals a **potential real difference** in the collision cooldown. The ROM `do_coll_l` does `DEC collcount; BEQ apply` (decrement *then* check zero), while the Rust does `if collcount > 0 { -=1; return }` (check *then* decrement). Those have different timing. Let me confirm the field offsets and oracle-test `do_coll_l` to settle it:
----------------------------------------

1. **Cooldown**: ROM does `DEC collcount; BNE exit` (decrement-then-check); Rust does `if collcount>0 {-=1; return}` (check-then-decrement) — off-by-one timing + different `collcount==0` handling.
2. **Indestructible**: ROM `LDA hp; BMI skip` treats **any `hp>=0x80`** (bit 7 set) as indestructible; Rust only checks `hp==0xFF`.

Let me oracle-confirm both before changing anything. Finding `tpa` (the collcount reset source):
----------------------------------------

### Turn 799 @ 2026-07-04T14:42:37.325Z
USER:
=== Rust aabb_overlap ===
pub fn aabb_overlap(
    x1: i16, y1: i16, z1: i16, e1x: i16, e1y: i16, e1z: i16,
    x2: i16, y2: i16, z2: i16, e2x: i16, e2y: i16, e2z: i16,
) -> bool {
    let mut dz = z2.wrapping_sub(z1);
    if dz < 0 {
        dz = dz.wrapping_neg();
    }
    if dz >= e1z.wrapping_add(e2z) {
        return false;
    }
    let mut dx = x2.wrapping_sub(x1);
    if dx < 0 {
        dx = dx.wrapping_neg();
    }
    if dx >= e1x.wrapping_add(e2x) {
        return false;
    }
    let mut dy = y2.wrapping_sub(y1);
    if dy < 0 {
        dy = dy.wrapping_neg();
    }
    if dy >= e1y.wrapping_add(e2y) {
        return false;
    }
    true
}

### Turn 811 @ 2026-07-04T14:47:01.598Z

pub fn strat_speed_to(al: &mut Alien, target_speed: u8, rate: u8) -> bool {
    if al.vel == target_speed {
        return true;
    }
    if al.vel < target_speed {
        al.vel = al.vel.wrapping_add(rate);
        if al.vel > target_speed {
            al.vel = target_speed;
        }
    } else if al.vel - target_speed < rate {
        al.vel = target_speed;
    } else {
        al.vel -= rate;
    }
    al.vel == target_speed
}
// ============================================================
// Percentage scaling (C strat_common.c:84-107, STRATROU.ASM perc*)
// ============================================================
/// C `Strat_Perc75`: val * 3/4 = val/2 + val/4 (arithmetic shifts).
pub fn strat_perc75(val: i16) -> i16 {
    (val >> 1).wrapping_add(val >> 2)
}
/// C `Strat_Perc87`: val * 7/8.
pub fn strat_perc87(val: i16) -> i16 {
    (val >> 1).wrapping_add(val >> 2).wrapping_add(val >> 3)
}
/// C `Strat_Perc62`: val * 5/8.
=== AL_SPEED offset ===

ASSISTANT (final):
The ROM `SR_SPEEDTO` snaps when `|speed-target| < rate`; the Rust does `vel += rate` then clamps — which can **wrapping-overflow** for large `vel+rate` (e.g. vel=200, target=255, rate=100 → Rust wraps to 44, ROM snaps to 255). pub fn sf_random(vars: &mut GameVars) -> u16 {
    let v = vars
        .sv_u16(sv::RNDVAL)
        .wrapping_mul(91)
        .wrapping_add(0x61D7);

    fn sv_u16(&self, addr: u16) -> u16 {
        self.read_ext16(addr)
    }
    fn sv_i16(&self, addr: u16) -> i16 {
        self.read_ext16(addr) as i16
    }
    fn set_sv_u8(&mut self, addr: u16, v: u8) {
        self.write_ext8(addr, v);
    }
    fn set_sv_i8(&mut self, addr: u16, v: i8) {
        self.write_ext8(addr, v as u8);
    }
    fn set_sv_u16(&mut self, addr: u16, v: u16) {
        self.write_ext16(addr, v);
    }
    fn set_sv_i16(&mut self, addr: u16, v: i16) {
        self.write_ext16(addr, v as u16);
    }
}

### Turn 858 @ 2026-07-04T15:13:54.150Z
USER:
29	fn swb_random(s: &mut [u8; 4]) -> u8 {
30	    let de0 = s[0];
31	    let subs = [s[1], s[2], s[3], de0];
32	    let idxs = [1usize, 2, 3, 0];
33	    let mut a = de0 as i32;
34	    let mut carry = 0i32; // CLC
35	    for k in 0..4 {
36	        let raw = a - subs[k] as i32 - (1 - carry);
37	        carry = (raw >= 0) as i32;
38	        a = raw & 0xFF;
39	        s[idxs[k]] = a as u8;
40	    }
41	    s[0]
42	}
43	
44	#[test]
45	fn random_swb_matches_rom() {
46	    let syms = load_symbols();
47	    let (Some(&addr), Some(rom)) = (syms.get("RANDOM_L"), load_built_rom()) else {
48	        eprintln!("skip: no symbol/ROM");
49	        return;
50	    };
51	    for seed in [[1u8, 2, 3, 4], [0xAB, 0xCD, 0xEF, 0x12], [0, 0, 0, 0], [0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF]] {
52	        let romv = rom_seq(&rom, addr, seed, 8);
53	        let mut st = seed;
54	        let rustv: Vec<u8> = (0..8).map(|_| swb_random(&mut st)).collect();
55	        eprintln!("seed {seed:02x?}\n  ROM  {romv:02x?}\n  RUST {rustv:02x?}  {}",
56	            if romv == rustv { "ok" } else { "DIFF" });
57	        assert_eq!(romv, rustv, "SWB algorithm must match ROM RANDOM for seed {seed:02x?}");
58	    }
59	}

### Turn 861 @ 2026-07-04T15:15:01.096Z
USER:
=== lerp_cam_angle_f + set_view_lerp (full) ===
fn lerp_cam_angle_f(from: i16, to: i16, t: f32, big_jump: &mut bool) -> f32 {
    let a8 = (from & 0xFF) as i32;
    let b8 = (to & 0xFF) as i32;
    let mut diff = b8 - a8;
    if diff > 127 {
        diff -= 256;
    }
--- set_view_lerp big_jump handling ---
    pub fn set_view_lerp(&mut self, alpha: f32) {
        if !self.cam_valid {
            return;
        }
        let alpha = alpha.clamp(0.0, 1.0);
        let mut big_jump = false;
        let rx = lerp_cam_angle_f(self.cam_prev.rx, self.cam_curr.rx, alpha, &mut big_jump);
        let ry = lerp_cam_angle_f(self.cam_prev.ry, self.cam_curr.ry, alpha, &mut big_jump);
        let rz = lerp_cam_angle_f(self.cam_prev.rz, self.cam_curr.rz, alpha, &mut big_jump);
        let dx = fp16_to_float(self.cam_curr.x.wrapping_sub(self.cam_prev.x));
        let dy = fp16_to_float(self.cam_curr.y.wrapping_sub(self.cam_prev.y));
        let dz = fp16_to_float(self.cam_curr.z.wrapping_sub(self.cam_prev.z));
        // A displacement this large in one 20Hz tick is a teleport, not motion.
        if dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz > 600.0 * 600.0 {
            big_jump = true;
        }
        if big_jump {
            self.cam_prev = self.cam_curr;
            let c = self.cam_curr;
            self.build_view_matrix(c.x, c.y, c.z, c.rx, c.ry, c.rz);
            return;
        }
        // Wrapping add: the camera position follows the player's i16 world
        // coords scaled to FP16.16, which sit near i32::MAX and wrap at the
        // 16-bit world boundary (SNES modular coords). A plain add overflows
        // in debug when the camera advances near the wrap point.
        let x = self
            .cam_prev
            .x
            .wrapping_add(((self.cam_curr.x.wrapping_sub(self.cam_prev.x)) as f32 * alpha) as i32);
        let y = self
            .cam_prev
            .y
            .wrapping_add(((self.cam_curr.y.wrapping_sub(self.cam_prev.y)) as f32 * alpha) as i32);

ASSISTANT (final):

=== existing scripts/run.sh ===
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Canonical launcher for the Rust Star Fox HD binary.
#
# Assets and config are resolved cwd-relative (starfox.ini -> AssetDir=data),
# so this script always runs the binary FROM THE REPO ROOT. No CMake / build
# staging step is required — `data/` and `starfox.ini` are read in place.
#
# Run inside the nix devshell (provides SDL3 / libGL on LD_LIBRARY_PATH):
#   nix develop --command ./scripts/run.sh
# Extra args are forwarded to the binary (e.g. --config, --asset-root,
# --shader-dir). Env knobs (SF_HIDDEN, SF_AUTOPLAY, SF_MAX_TICKS,
# SF_DUMP_PPM, ...) are honoured as usual.
set -euo pipefail

REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$REPO_ROOT"

PROFILE="${SF_PROFILE:-debug}"
BIN="rust/target/${PROFILE}/starfox-hd-rs"

if [[ ! -x "$BIN" ]]; then
    echo "Building starfox-hd-rs ($PROFILE)..." >&2
    if [[ "$PROFILE" == "release" ]]; then
        ( cd rust && cargo build --release -p sf-app )
    else
        ( cd rust && cargo build -p sf-app )
    fi
fi

exec "$BIN" "$@"

=== existing gsu_arctan test (how a GSU routine is invoked) ===
//! End-to-end GSU validation: run the real `mcallarctan16` GSU program from the
//! ROM and check it computes atan2. This is what proves the GSU emulator is
//! trustworthy enough to validate GSU-side game functions (angle/3D math).
//!
//! ABI (from disassembling $01:81AA): inputs in GSU RAM (bank $70) m_x1=$62,
//! m_y1=$2C; result m_cnt=$40. arctan16 returns arctan(x1/y1), 0..359deg mapped
//! to $0000..$FFFF.
use sf_oracle::gsu::Gsu;
use sf_oracle::load_built_rom;
const M_X1: usize = 0x62;
const M_Y1: usize = 0x2C;
const M_CNT: usize = 0x40;
fn gsu_arctan(rom: &[u8], x: i16, y: i16) -> u16 {
    let mut gsu = Gsu::new(rom.to_vec());
    gsu.ram[M_X1] = x as u16 as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_X1 + 1] = (x as u16 >> 8) as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_Y1] = y as u16 as u8;
    gsu.ram[M_Y1 + 1] = (y as u16 >> 8) as u8;
    gsu.run(1, 0x81AA); // mcallarctan16 @ $01:81AA
    gsu.ram[M_CNT] as u16 | ((gsu.ram[M_CNT + 1] as u16) << 8)
}
fn expected_angle(x: i16, y: i16) -> u16 {
    let mut a = (x as f64).atan2(y as f64);
    if a < 0.0 {
        a += 2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI;
    }
    (a * 65536.0 / (2.0 * std::f64::consts::PI)).round() as u16
}

    let Some(rom) = load_built_rom() else {

    // core (prefix system, ~50 opcodes, memory, FMULT, MOVE/MOVES, LINK, LOOP,
    // branches, the GSU RAM/ROM ABI). Off-axis/shallow angles depend on the

fn build_view_matrix_f(&mut self, cx: i32, cy: i32, cz: i32, crx: f32, cry: f32, crz: f32) {
        self.cam_render = CameraState {
            x: cx,
            y: cy,
            z: cz,
            rx: crx.round() as i16,
            ry: cry.round() as i16,
            rz: crz.round() as i16,
        };
        self.cam_render_pitch_f = signed_angle_f(crx);
        self.cam_render_yaw_f = signed_angle_f(cry);
        // Build view matrix from camera position and SNES rotation angles.
        // SNES -> GL world: negate Y translation, negate X/Z rotation angles.
        let cy = -cy;
        let (sx, cx_) = self.sincos_frac(-crx);
        let (sy, cy_) = self.sincos_frac(cry);
        let (sz, cz_) = self.sincos_frac(-crz);
        // ZYX rotation order (matching SNES)
        let mut rotation = [0.0f32; 16];
        identity(&mut rotation);
        rotation[0] = cy_ * cz_;
        rotation[1] = cy_ * sz;
        rotation[2] = -sy;
        rotation[4] = sx * sy * cz_ - cx_ * sz;
        rotation[5] = sx * sy * sz + cx_ * cz_;
        rotation[6] = sx * cy_;
        rotation[8] = cx_ * sy * cz_ + sx * sz;
        rotation[9] = cx_ * sy * sz - sx * cz_;
        rotation[10] = cx_ * cy_;
        // Translation (negate for view matrix)
        let tx = -fp16_to_float(cx);
        let ty = -fp16_to_float(cy);
        let tz = -fp16_to_float(cz);
        // View = Rotation^T * Translation
        identity(&mut self.view);
        for i in 0..3 {
            for j in 0..3 {
                self.view[j * 4 + i] = rotation[i * 4 + j];
            }
        }
        // Facing conversion: the SNES camera looks down +Z, the OpenGL
        // camera looks down -Z. Negate the view-space Z row so
        // world-forward (+Z) maps to GL view-forward (-Z).
        self.view[2] = -self.view[2];
        self.view[6] = -self.view[6];
        self.view[10] = -self.view[10];
        // Apply translation in rotated space
        self.view[12] = self.view[0] * tx + self.view[4] *

### Turn 1008 @ 2026-07-04T20:45:38.468Z
USER:

=== sincos_frac convention ===
    fn sincos_frac(&self, a: f32) -> (f32, f32) {
        let a = a.rem_euclid(256.0);
        let i0 = a.floor() as usize % 256;
        let i1 = (i0 + 1) % 256;
        let f = a - a.floor();
        let s = self.sin_table[i0] + (self.sin_table[i1] - self.sin_table[i0]) * f;
        let c = self.cos_table[i0] + (self.cos_table[i1] - self.cos_table[i0]) * f;
        (s, c)
    }

ASSISTANT (final):
`sincos_frac(a)` = standard sin/cos for an 8-bit angle. So the fix uses positive angles + the proven `Ry·Rx·Rz` expansion. Let me apply it to `build_view_matrix_f`:

/// C `Strat_DistXZ` (xzdiffs_l): approximate Manhattan distance in the XZ
/// plane. Widths mirror the C int-promotion then int16 truncation.
pub fn strat_dist_xz(a: &Alien, b: &Alien) -> i16 {
    let dx = ((b.worldx as i32 - a.worldx as i32).abs()) as i16;
    let dz = ((b.worldz as i32 - a.worldz as i32).abs()) as i16;
    dx.wrapping_add(dz)
}

ASSISTANT (final):
Scoping confirms Agent 2's finding precisely. The ROM's normal-flight camera uses the **view accumulators**, not the ship's rotation:
- `viewrotx = outvx` (≈0, level) — but the Rust uses `player.rotx` (follows ship pitch)
- `viewroty = outvy - player_turnrot` (≈ **−turnrot**, follows heading) — but the Rust uses `player.roty - turnrot`, which **cancels** to ≈0

So the camera currently follows ship *pitch* (shouldn't) and ignores *turns* (should follow). Fixing it is oracle-proven correct — but it's a **major camera-feel change** to the exact behavior you were sensitive about, so I want your call before applying it rather than surprising you.

/// C `zacos3_strat` (GASTRATS.ASM:981-996) — attack-dive laser barrage.
/// Each frame `rotx -= 4` (full 256-step loop). Fires RELSLOWELASER on the
/// frame rotx wraps to 0, otherwise every 4th frame while pitch is in the
/// forward arc (rotx not minus and not below -deg90). Transitions to zacos4
/// only after rotx wraps back to -4 (0xFC).
fn zacos_phase2(g: &mut Game, idx: u16) {
    let rotx = {
        let al = &mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        al.rotx = al.rotx.wrapping_sub(4); // s_add_alvar B,x,al_rotx,#-4
        al.rotx
    };
    // Fire gate (GASTRATS.ASM:986-989).
    let fire = if rotx == 0 {
        true // s_beq .fire
    } else if rotx & 0x80 != 0 {
        false // s_jmp_alvarmi -> .nfire
    } else if rotx.wrapping_sub(DEG90.wrapping_neg()) & 0x80 != 0 {
        false // s_jmp_alvarless #-deg90 -> .nfire
    } else {
        g.vars.gameframe & 3 == 0 // s_jmp_notdelay 2 -> .nfire
    };
    if fire {
        // s_weapon_rot #0,#0 -> fire forward relative to object orientation.
        let me = g.objs.aliens[idx as usize];
        strat_fire_relslowlaser(g, idx, me.rotx, me.roty);
    }
    if rotx == 4u8.wrapping_neg() {
        // s_jmp_alvarEQ B,x,al_rotx,#-4,zacos4_init
        let s = sid(g, zacos_phase3);
        g.objs.aliens[idx as usize].stratptr = Some(s);
    }
    zacos_move(g, idx);
}

/// C `zacos4_strat` (GASTRATS.ASM:1001-1007) — s_speedto #60,1 + s_banktoplayer.
fn zacos_phase3(g: &mut Game, idx: u16) {
    let _ = speed_to(&mut g.objs.aliens[idx as usize], 60, 1);
    szaco2_bank_to_player(g, idx);
    zacos_move(g, idx);
}
